# Recovery Workbench — AI engineering standards (v2)

This is the standards file the coding agent reads before every task, as defined in
`Recovery Workbench AI Assisted Development Approach v1` (section 3). It applies to the recovery
module of the ACE service and to anyone using an AI tool on this repository.

**Owner:** Solution architect with the TechX tech leads.
**Change control:** pull request, reviewed like code.
**Baseline:** document set v14, 16 November 2026. The implementation evidence from 10 November 2026
(`RWB-E1-042`, `RWB-TE-001`) is preserved; both are re-specified against this baseline.
**Source:** `requirement/legacy/08-ai-and-agents/recovery-workbench-ai-assisted-development-approach-v1.md`
and `requirement/legacy/11-delivery-and-operations/recovery-workbench-engineering-practices-v5-docx.md`.

## 1. The seven working rules

1. The person who merges a change owns it, whoever or whatever wrote it.
2. The agent works from files: the standards file, the story file and the plan file. Anything the
   agent needs is in one of the three.
3. The tests and the acceptance criteria exist before the code is generated.
4. Every line that reaches production is read by a person.
5. An AI error found by a reviewer is written into this file, so the agent is told not to repeat it.
6. No personal data or production data goes into a prompt, a test or a log.
7. The unit of work is one story. Work is merged at least once a working day.

## 2. Structure and stack

- The Workbench is the recovery module of the ACE service: one service, one repository, one front end.
- Back end: Spring Boot (Java 21), Spring JPA, Spring Batch, Spring Security with method security,
  Spring Session, Spring Cloud Azure, Spring Data REST, Spring HATEOAS and Quartz.
- Front end: React on Vite with Radix and TanStack Query. Front-end types are generated from the
  back end.
- Database: PostgreSQL, schema `recovery` in the ACE database, referenced only as
  `${recoverySchema}` and fixed to `recovery` in every environment (D-RCV-28). Schema changes use
  versioned migrations, one migration per story, forward-only. Files are named
  `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`, where `<seq>` is a four-digit sequence taken in merge order and line 1
  is `-- Story: <story key>`.
- Domain driven design, RESTful API, end-to-end type safety and validation.
- Cases are modelled in CMMN 1.1 terms: case, stage, milestone, task, sentry, event listener and
  case file item. From Epic 3 the case lifecycle runs on Temporal; case data stays in the recovery
  schema (D-RCV-36, ADR-011).
- Rules are versioned data, authored as Drools XLS decision-table packages and evaluated by embedded
  Drools (D-RCV-36, ADR-012).
- Hosting: the ACE namespace on the shared ACE AKS clusters; Temporal is self-hosted in the same
  namespace.

## 3. The agent must

1. Put recovery code in the recovery module folder only. Change the common or collection module only
   where the story file names it.
2. Read every threshold, limit, timer, queue, role, template and route from its versioned reference
   table. Never write one as a literal in code.
3. Put a Spring Security method check on every endpoint, naming the capability area and the operation.
4. Write a decision record entry for every change to a case and every decision, with the rule ID and
   version used.
5. Use the names in the business glossary for classes, fields, tables and endpoints.
6. Return each validation message from the message table by its rule ID.
7. Make database changes by migration script only, one script per story. Name it
   `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`; line 1 is `-- Story: <story key>`. A corrective script takes the
   next sequence, keeps the original story key and adds line 2 `-- Fix: <reason>` (D-RCV-28).
8. Use Spring Batch and Quartz for every Epic 1...

```text
# Recovery Workbench, local build of the ACE recovery module.
# Profile "local" (application-local.yaml) points at the docker compose stack.
# SIT/UAT/PROD values come from the ACE deployment and Key Vault; nothing secret lives here.

spring:
  application:
    name: ace-recovery
  threads:
    virtual:
      enabled: true
  datasource:
    url: ${RWB_DB_URL:jdbc:postgresql://localhost:5433/ace}
    username: ${RWB_DB_USER:recovery_svc}
    password: ${RECOVERY_SVC_PASSWORD:}
    hikari:
      maximum-pool-size: 50          # NFR: 180 concurrent users, pool 50
      connection-init-sql: SET TIME ZONE 'Asia/Bangkok'
  jpa:
    open-in-view: false              # BS-3.6: no lazy loading outside a transaction
    hibernate:
      ddl-auto: none
    properties:
      hibernate.default_schema: ${rwb.schema}
      hibernate.jdbc.time_zone: Asia/Bangkok
  flyway:
    enabled: false                   # the recovery module runs its own Flyway bean (BS-2.3)
  quartz:
    job-store-type: memory           # ACE uses its JDBC job store; locally Quartz is in memory
  jackson:
    time-zone: Asia/Bangkok

server:
  port: 8080
  shutdown: graceful
  error:
    include-stacktrace: never
    include-message: never

management:
  endpoints:
    web:
      exposure:
        include: health, info, prometheus, metrics
  endpoint:
    health:
      probes:
        enabled: true
      show-details: when-authorized
      roles: RW-PLATADM
  tracing:
    sampling:
      probability: 1.0
  opentelemetry:
    tracing:
      export:
        otlp:
          endpoint: ${RWB_OTLP_TRACES_ENDPOINT:http://localhost:4318/v1/traces}
  otlp:
    metrics:
      export:

```

```text
#!/bin/sh
# Local only. Builds the ace database from the document set v14 sources in /schema:
#
#   01-ace-schema-v1.sql                 ACE public schema (62 tables). The ACE team owns it.
#   02-ace-tier2-probe.sql               Probe DDL for the RWB-ACE-001 Tier 2 additions (14 tables,
#                                        12 columns). Not a deliverable; the ACE team owns the real
#                                        change. RWB_ACE_TIER2_PROBE=false leaves it out, and the 14
#                                        Tier 2 views then fail to compile, as they would in SIT today.
#   03-recovery-physical-schema-v6.sql   Recovery Physical Schema v6: 93 tables, 30 views, dictionary
#                                        and seed reference data. Stands in for V0001-V0003 until
#                                        RWB-E1-001 ships them as Flyway migrations. Set
#                                        RWB_LOAD_REFERENCE_SCHEMA=false once it does, and Flyway
#                                        builds the schema instead.
set -eu

run() {
  echo "rwb-init: applying $1"
  psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP="$2" --quiet --username "$POSTGRES_USER" --dbname ace -f "/schema/$1"
}

run 01-ace-schema-v1.sql 1

if [ "${RWB_ACE_TIER2_PROBE:-true}" = "true" ]; then
  run 02-ace-tier2-probe.sql 1
  strict=1
else
  echo "rwb-init: RWB_ACE_TIER2_PROBE=false, Tier 2 views will not compile"
  strict=0
fi

if [ "${RWB_LOAD_REFERENCE_SCHEMA:-true}" = "true" ]; then
  run 03-recovery-physical-schema-v6.sql "$strict"
else
  echo "rwb-init: RWB_LOAD_REFERENCE_SCHEMA=false, recovery schema left to Flyway"
  psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 --username "$POSTGRES_USER" --dbname ace \
    -c "CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS recovery; CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pgcrypto;"
fi

echo "rwb-init: applying ownership and grants"
psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 --username "$POSTGRES_USER" --dbname ace -f /docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/20-grants.sql.inc

psql --username "$POSTGRES_USER" --dbname ace -tAc "
  SELECT 'rwb-init: public tables='   || count(*) FILTER (WHERE table_schema = 'public'   AND table_type = 'BASE TABLE')
      || ', recovery tables='         || count(*) FILTER (WHERE table_schema = 'recovery' AND table_type = 'BASE TABLE')
      || ', v_ace views='             || count(*) FILTER (WHERE table_schema = 'recovery' AND table_type = 'VIEW' AND table_name LIKE 'v\_ace\_%')
  FROM information_schema.tables"
=== ENV ===
```

```text
-- Local only. Run by 10-load-ace-database.sh, not by the Postgres entrypoint (hence .sql.inc).
-- Applies the ADR-010 read boundary in the catalog, so a direct read of a public table by the
-- recovery service fails at the database and not only in code review.

-- The recovery schema and its tables belong to the migration account.
ALTER SCHEMA recovery OWNER TO rwb_migrator;
GRANT USAGE, CREATE ON SCHEMA recovery TO ace_view_owner;   -- CREATE is needed to own the views

-- ace_view_owner reads ACE; nobody else does.
REVOKE ALL ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC;
GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO ace_view_owner;
GRANT SELECT ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public TO ace_view_owner;

DO $$
DECLARE
  r record;
BEGIN
  -- Tables, sequences and non-ACE views: rwb_migrator
  FOR r IN SELECT c.relname, c.relkind FROM pg_class c JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = c.relnamespace
           WHERE n.nspname = 'recovery' AND c.relkind IN ('r', 'p', 'v')
             AND NOT (c.relkind = 'v' AND c.relname LIKE 'v\_ace\_%') LOOP
    EXECUTE format('ALTER TABLE recovery.%I OWNER TO rwb_migrator', r.relname);
  END LOOP;

  -- Functions: rwb_migrator, executable by the view owner and the service
  FOR r IN SELECT p.oid::regprocedure AS sig FROM pg_proc p JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = p.pronamespace
           WHERE n.nspname = 'recovery' LOOP
    EXECUTE format('ALTER FUNCTION %s OWNER TO rwb_migrator', r.sig);
  END LOOP;

  -- v_ace_* views: ace_view_owner, SELECT only for recovery_svc
  FOR r IN SELECT c.relname FROM pg_class c JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = c.relnamespace
           WHERE n.nspname = 'recovery' AND c.relkind = 'v' AND c.relname LIKE 'v\_ace\_%' LOOP
    EXECUTE format('ALTER VIEW recovery.%I OWNER TO ace_view_owner', r.relname);
    EXECUTE format('REVOKE ALL ON recovery.%I FROM recovery_svc', r.relname);
    EXECUTE format('GRANT SELECT ON recovery.%I TO recovery_svc', r.relname);
  END LOOP;
END $$;

GRANT EXECUTE ON ALL FUNCTIONS IN SCHEMA recovery TO ace_view_owner, recovery_svc;

-- recovery_svc: DML on Workbench tables (not on the ACE views)
GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA recovery TO recovery_svc;
DO $$
DECLARE
  r record;
BEGIN
  FOR r IN SELECT c.relname FROM pg_class c JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = c.relnamespace
           WHERE n.nspname = 'recovery' AND c.relkind IN ('r', 'p') LOOP
    EXECUTE format('GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON recovery.%I TO recovery_svc', r.relname);
  END LOOP;
END $$;
GRANT USAGE
```

```text
28: ## 1. Story
29: 
30: As a **data steward and data product owner (RW-DATASTEW)**, I want **the 92 recovery tables for the 14 data products created in the ACE database, with a data dictionary that covers those tables and the 30 read-only ACE views, and one system of record and one owner per data product**, so that **every Workbench record has one home and every data product has one accountable owner and one read path**.
31: 
32: ## 2. Business rationale
33: 
34: The Workbench holds no copy of source data. ACE ingests ALS, SCBPDM, EFS, COLLINS, CCMS, SVAF, AMIS, RPS, LIT, WIN, GL, NLEADS, EasyApp and Entra ID, and the Workbench reads what it needs through the 30 `recovery.v_ace_*` views (D-RCV-35 U1 and U4, ADR-010). This story creates the 88 Workbench tables, the four extension tables that hold a Workbench decision about an ACE record, the metadata registry that holds the data dictionary as data, and the catalog of the 14 data products DP01 to DP14. Each data product then has exactly one system of record, written "ACE (ingested from X)" for source data, "Recovery Workbench" for Workbench records, or one sentence naming both where the product holds both, and one named owner, as REQ-RDF-001 requires. The measure it serves is "the ACE batch is complete when the Workbench reads it on 99 percent of days", because the dictionary and the catalog are what every later read and every freshness stamp are resolved against.
```

Instructions from: C:\Users\SahilMore\Downloads\Recovery Workbench SharePoint Set v14\17 AI-First SDLC Repository v2\AGENTS.md
# Recovery Workbench — AI engineering standards (v2)

This is the standards file the coding agent reads before every task, as defined in
`Recovery Workbench AI Assisted Development Approach v1` (section 3). It applies to the recovery
module of the ACE service and to anyone using an AI tool on this repository.

**Owner:** Solution architect with the TechX tech leads.
**Change control:** pull request, reviewed like code.
**Baseline:** document set v14, 16 November 2026. The implementation evidence from 10 November 2026
(`RWB-E1-042`, `RWB-TE-001`) is preserved; both are re-specified against this baseline.
**Source:** `requirement/legacy/08-ai-and-agents/recovery-workbench-ai-assisted-development-approach-v1.md`
and `requirement/legacy/11-delivery-and-operations/recovery-workbench-engineering-practices-v5-docx.md`.

## 1. The seven working rules

1. The person who merges a change owns it, whoever or whatever wrote it.
2. The agent works from files: the standards file, the story file and the plan file. Anything the
   agent needs is in one of the three.
3. The tests and the acceptance criteria exist before the code is generated.
4. Every line that reaches production is read by a person.
5. An AI error found by a reviewer is written into this file, so the agent is told not to repeat it.
6. No personal data or production data goes into a prompt, a test or a log.
7. The unit of work is one story. Work is merged at least once a working day.

## 2. Structure and stack

- The Workbench is the recovery module of the ACE service: one service, one repository, one front end.
- Back end: Spring Boot (Java 21), Spring JPA, Spring Batch, Spring Security with method security,
  Spring Session, Spring Cloud Azure, Spring Data REST, Spring HATEOAS and Quartz.
- Front end: React on Vite with Radix and TanStack Query. Front-end types are generated from the
  back end.
- Database: PostgreSQL, schema `recovery` in the ACE database, referenced only as
  `${recoverySchema}` and fixed to `recovery` in every environment (D-RCV-28). Schema changes use
  versioned migrations, one migration per story, forward-only. Files are named
  `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`, where `<seq>` is a four-digit sequence taken in merge order and line 1
  is `-- Story: <story key>`.
- Domain driven design, RESTful API, end-to-end type safety and validation.
- Cases are modelled in CMMN 1.1 terms: case, stage, milestone, task, sentry, event listener and
  case file item. From Epic 3 the case lifecycle runs on Temporal; case data stays in the recovery
  schema (D-RCV-36, ADR-011).
- Rules are versioned data, authored as Drools XLS decision-table packages and evaluated by embedded
  Drools (D-RCV-36, ADR-012).
- Hosting: the ACE namespace on the shared ACE AKS clusters; Temporal is self-hosted in the same
  namespace.

## 3. The agent must

1. Put recovery code in the recovery module folder only. Change the common or collection module only
   where the story file names it.
2. Read every threshold, limit, timer, queue, role, template and route from its versioned reference
   table. Never write one as a literal in code.
3. Put a Spring Security method check on every endpoint, naming the capability area and the operation.
4. Write a decision record entry for every change to a case and every decision, with the rule ID and
   version used.
5. Use the names in the business glossary for classes, fields, tables and endpoints.
6. Return each validation message from the message table by its rule ID.
7. Make database changes by migration script only, one script per story. Name it
   `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`; line 1 is `-- Story: <story key>`. A corrective script takes the
   next sequence, keeps the original story key and adds line 2 `-- Fix: <reason>` (D-RCV-28).
8. Use Spring Batch and Quartz for every E...

```text
2151 
2152 -- References between tables
2153 
2154 -- v_data_dictionary: the data dictionary as one row per field, joining the metadata tables to
2155 -- the data product catalog so a developer can ask where a field comes from and who owns it.
2156 CREATE VIEW v_data_dictionary AS
2157 SELECT o.object_name      AS entity,
2158        f.field_name       AS field,
2159        f.field_id,
2160        f.data_type,
2161        f.physical_type,
2162        f.is_mandatory,
2163        f.lov_code,
2164        f.references_object,
2165        f.source_system,
2166        f.source_field,
2167        f.is_personal,
2168        o.data_product,
2169        p.refresh_cycle,
2170        f.owner_role,
2171        p.owner_user_id,
2172        f.late_behavior,
2173        f.interface_id,
2174        f.description
2175 FROM sys_fields f
2176 JOIN sys_objects o ON o.object_name = f.object_name
2177 LEFT JOIN data_product p ON p.product_code = o.data_product AND p.is_active
2178 WHERE f.is_active AND o.is_active;
2179 
2180 COMMENT ON VIEW v_data_dictionary IS 'The data dictionary as one row per field, with its data product, refresh cycle and owner.';

```

```text
=== data_product
CREATE TABLE data_product (
    id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    product_code varchar(25) NOT NULL,
    product_name varchar(80) NOT NULL,
    definition varchar(400) NOT NULL,
    system_of_record varchar(120) NOT NULL,
    owner_role varchar(20) NOT NULL,
    owner_user_id uuid,
    refresh_cycle varchar(10) NOT NULL,
    expected_by varchar(20),
    valid_from date NOT NULL,
    is_active boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
    state varchar(40),
    version integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
    created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    created_by uuid,
    updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_by uuid,
    CONSTRAINT uk_data_product UNIQUE (product_code)
);
=== sys_objects
CREATE TABLE sys_objects (
    id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    object_name varchar(60) NOT NULL,
    business_name varchar(120) NOT NULL,
    subject_area varchar(10) NOT NULL,
    data_product varchar(10),
    system_of_record varchar(120) NOT NULL,
```

```text
# Recovery Workbench, local end-to-end stack.
#
#   docker compose up -d                  platform only: Postgres, Temporal, Azurite, mock Entra ID
#   docker compose --profile app up -d    platform plus the backend and frontend containers
#
# Local stand-ins for the bank estate (see README, "Local versus target"):
#   postgres     ACE database (public + recovery) and the two Temporal databases
#   temporal     self-hosted Temporal (target: official Helm chart on ACE AKS, ADR-011)
#   azurite      Azure Blob storage for the document hub (ADR-007)
#   mock-oidc    Entra ID OIDC (target: bank Entra ID tenant with MFA)

name: rwb-e2e

x-postgres-env: &pg-env
  POSTGRES_HOST: postgres
  POSTGRES_PORT: "5432"

services:
  postgres:
    image: postgres:16.16-alpine
    environment:
      POSTGRES_USER: ${POSTGRES_SUPERUSER:-postgres}
      POSTGRES_PASSWORD: ${POSTGRES_SUPERUSER_PASSWORD:?set in .env}
      RWB_MIGRATOR_PASSWORD: ${RWB_MIGRATOR_PASSWORD:?set in .env}
      RECOVERY_SVC_PASSWORD: ${RECOVERY_SVC_PASSWORD:?set in .env}
      TEMPORAL_SVC_PASSWORD: ${TEMPORAL_SVC_PASSWORD:?set in .env}
      RWB_ACE_TIER2_PROBE: ${RWB_ACE_TIER2_PROBE:-true}
      RWB_LOAD_REFERENCE_SCHEMA: ${RWB_LOAD_REFERENCE_SCHEMA:-true}
      TZ: Asia/Bangkok
    ports:
      - "${POSTGRES_HOST_PORT:-5433}:5432"
    volumes:
      - pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql/data
      - ./infra/postgres/init:/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d:ro
      - ./infra/postgres/schema:/schema:ro
    healthcheck:
      # TCP, not the socket: the init-time server listens on the socket only, so this stays
      # unhealthy until the schema build has finished.
      test: ["CMD-SHELL", "pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -U $${POSTGRES_USER} -d ace"]
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 60
      start_period: 30s

    image: temporalio/auto-setup:1.29.7
    depends_on:
      postgres:
        condition: service_healthy
    environment:
      DB: postgres12
      DB_PORT: "5432"
      POSTGRES_SEEDS: postgres
      POSTGRES_USER: temporal_svc
      POSTGRES_PWD: ${TEMPORAL_SVC_PASSWORD:?set in .env}
      DBNAME: temporal
      VISIBILITY_DBNAME: temporal_visibility
      SKIP_DB_CREATE: "true"
      DEFAULT_NAMESPACE: rwb-recovery
      DEFAULT_NAMESPACE_RETENTION: 30d
      DYNAMIC_CONFIG_FILE_PATH: config/dynamicconfig/development-sql.yaml
    ports:
      - "7233:7233"
    volumes:
      - ./infra/temporal/development-sql.yaml:/et
```

```text
# Recovery migrations

Flyway location for the recovery module. History table `flyway_schema_history_recovery`.

Rules (D-RCV-28, BS-2.2 to BS-2.6, checked by `RecoveryMigrationLintTest`):

- File name `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`, 4-digit contiguous sequence taken at merge, for example
  `V0001__rwb_e1_001_recovery_schema.sql`.
- Line 1 `-- Story: <story key>`. A corrective file takes the next sequence, keeps the story key and
  adds line 2 `-- Fix: <reason>`.
- Always `${recoverySchema}`, never a literal `recovery.`, `ace.` or `public.`; no `SET search_path`.
- No `R__` files, no `GRANT`/`REVOKE`, never edit a merged file.

The folder is empty on purpose. Locally the schema comes from Physical Schema v6, loaded by
`infra/postgres/init/10-load-ace-database.sh`. When RWB-E1-001 ships V0001 to V0003, set
`RWB_LOAD_REFERENCE_SCHEMA=false` and Flyway builds the schema from here.

```
